The return value is the manifest plus a ``manifest`` path key.\r\n  10: * ``restore_database(backup, expected_sha256, restore_target)`` is the resume surface, and it is a\r\n  11:   *gate* rather than an overwriting writer. It refuses a digest mismatch with\r\n  12:   ``ERR_BACKUP_DIGEST_MISMATCH`` before it touches the filesystem, and it refuses an existing target\r\n  13:   with ``ERR_RESTORE_TARGET_EXISTS``. That second refusal is how \"a resume must not repeat a world\r\n  14:   effect\" is enforced here: replaying a verified restore does not rewrite, merge into, or accumulate\r\n  15:   onto the target -- it is rejected, and the target's bytes stay byte-identical.\r\n  16:   The replay test therefore asserts the *refusal plus byte equality* guarantee that the code\r\n  17:   documents, not an in-place idempotent rewrite that ``restore_database`` does not implement. If the\r\n  18:   implementation ever learns to re-restore in place, that test fails loudly and the guarantee has to\r\n  19:   be restated deliberately instead of silently drifting.\r\n  20: * A successful restore returns ``RESTORE_READBACK_PASS`` with the sha256 it measured *after* the\r\n  21:   copy, and a copy that fails ``PRAGMA integrity_check`` is unlinked before\r\n  22:   ``ERR_RESTORE_INTEGRITY`` is raised -- so a failed restore cannot leave a half-written target\r\n  23:   standing in for a committed checkpoint.\r\n  24: * An interrupted restore is modelled the way a killed copy really leaves the world: the target path\r\n  25:   is *occupied* by what the dead attempt produced -- a truncated file, or a directory wearing the\r\n  26:   target's name -- and the module reports it as ``ERR_RESTORE_TARGET_EXISTS`` instead of writing\r\n  27:   through it. That is a different situation from an absent target, and the resume test pins it.\r\n  28: * The module has no ``operation_id``, no readback after a timeout, and no UNKNOWN/PARTIAL\r\n  29:   reconciliation (KB ``SBL_HGK_FABRIC_PIPD_LS_PKG_R15_1...#1418``).\r\n  30:   ``test_resume_requires_operation_identity`` states that contract and is marked\r\n  31:   ``xfail(strict=True)``: it fails today, so the gap stays visible, and it turns XPASS -- and\r\n  32:   therefore a failing build -- the moment ``recovery`` grows the identity, so the rule cannot be\r\n  33:   quietly satisfied by a target.read_bytes() == first_bytes, \"the refused replay changed the target's bytes\"\r\n 174:     assert sorted(entry.name for entry in target.parent.iterdir()) == [\"spine.db\"]\r\n 175:     assert _rows(target) == PAYLOADS, \"the refused replay duplicated or dropped rows\"\r\n 176: \r\n 177: \r\n 178: def test_resume_with_mismatched_digest_is_refused_and_target_untouched(tmp_path):\r\n 179:     \"\"\"A resume whose expected digest disagrees with the backup is refused and writes nothing.\r\n 180: \r\n 181:     Two cases are pinned, because \"untouched\" means different things for each: a target.write_bytes(sentinel)\r\n 196:     before_digest = _sha256(target)\r\n 197: \r\n 198:     with pytest.raises(InvariantViolation) as caught:\r\n 199:         recovery.restore_database(backup, it is still the accepted digest.\r\n 215:     assert _sha256(backup) == receipt[\"sha256\"]\r\n 216: \r\n 217: \r\n 218: def test_resume_after_interrupted_restore_reproduces_the_backup_exactly(tmp_path):\r\n 219:     \"\"\"After an interrupted restore, resuming from the intact backup reproduces its bytes exactly.\r\n 220: \r\n 221:     The interruption is not \"the target never existed\". A copy that dies part-way leaves the target\r\n 222:     path *occupied* by its own debris, and this test produces both shapes of that debris: a file\r\n 223:     holding a strict prefix of the backup (a killed ``copy2``), and a directory sitting on the\r\n 224:     target name with a partial payload inside (a crashed extraction). In each case the module\r\n 225:     reports the occupied destination -- ``ERR_RESTORE_TARGET_EXISTS`` -- and leaves the debris\r\n 226:     byte-identical, so the resume cannot merge into it, append to it, or silently treat a\r\n 227:     half-written file as a checkpoint. Once the failed attempt's debris is cleared, the resume has\r\n 228:     to reproduce the backup: the load-bearing assertions are byte equality with the backup file,\r\n 229:     its digest, ``PRAGMA integrity_check``, and each row present exactly once -- not any message.\r\n 230:     \"\"\"\r\n 231:     database = _seeded_database(tmp_path)\r\n 232:     receipt = recovery.backup_database(database, tmp_path / \"backups\")\r\n 233:     backup = Path(receipt[\"backup\"])\r\n 234:     backup_digest = _sha256(backup)\r\n 235:     backup_bytes = backup.read_bytes()\r\n 236:     assert backup_digest == receipt[\"sha256\"]\r\n 237: \r\n 238:     # --- interruption 1: a copy killed mid-file leaves a truncated target behind ------------------\r\n 239:     target = tmp_path / \"restore\" / \"spine.db\"\r\n 240:     target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\r\n 241:     interrupted_bytes = backup_bytes[: len(backup_bytes) // 2]\r\n 242:     assert 0 < len(interrupted_bytes) < len(backup_bytes), (\r\n 243:         \"the interruption is not a truncated copy\"\r\n 244:     )\r\n 245:     assert backup_bytes.startswith(interrupted_bytes), (\r\n 246:         \"the interruption is not a prefix of the backup\"\r\n 247:     )\r\n 248:     target.write_bytes(interrupted_bytes)\r\n 249: \r\n 250:     with pytest.raises(InvariantViolation) as caught:\r\n 251:         recovery.restore_database(backup, backup_digest, target)\r\n 252:     assert str(caught.value).startswith(\"ERR_RESTORE_TARGET_EXISTS\"), (\r\n 253:         \"the product did not report the interrupted attempt's leftover; a resume that writes \"\r\n 254:         \"through it would be the repeated world effect this test exists to catch\"\r\n 255:     )\r\n 256:     assert target.read_bytes() == interrupted_bytes, (\r\n 257:         \"the refused resume wrote through the interrupted copy\"\r\n 258:     )\r\n 259:     assert _sha256(target) != backup_digest, (\r\n 260:         \"the interrupted target already looks like a finished restore\"\r\n 261:     )\r\n 262: \r\n 263:     # The dead attempt's artifact is cleared; the resume now has to reproduce the checkpoint.\r\n 264:     target.unlink()\r\n 265:     assert not target.exists()\r\n 266:     assert backup.is_file(), \"the interrupted restore damaged the backup\"\r\n 267:     assert _sha256(backup) == backup_digest\r\n 268: \r\n 269:     resumed = recovery.restore_database(backup, backup_digest, target)\r\n 270: \r\n 271:     assert resumed[\"status\"] == \"RESTORE_READBACK_PASS\"\r\n 272:     assert resumed[\"sha256\"] == backup_digest\r\n 273:     assert target.read_bytes() == backup_bytes, (\r\n 274:         \"the resumed restore is not byte-identical to the backup\"\r\n 275:     )\r\n 276:     assert _sha256(target) == backup_digest\r\n 277: \r\n 278:     connection = sqlite3.connect(target)\r\n 279:     try:\r\n 280:         integrity = connection.execute(\"PRAGMA integrity_check\").fetchone()[0]\r\n 281:     finally:\r\n 282:         connection.close()\r\n 283:     assert integrity == \"ok\"\r\n 284:     assert _rows(target) == PAYLOADS, (\r\n 285:         \"the resumed checkpoint re-applied rows instead of reproducing them\"\r\n 286:     )\r\n 287:     assert len(_rows(target)) == len(PAYLOADS)\r\n 288: \r\n 289:     # --- interruption 2: the crashed attempt left a directory on the target name ------------------\r\n 290:     blocked = tmp_path / \"restore-dir\" / \"spine.db\"\r\n 291:     blocked.mkdir(parents=True)\r\n 292:     leftover = blocked / \"partial.tmp\"\r\n 293:     leftover_bytes = backup_bytes[: 1024]\r\n 294:     leftover.write_bytes(leftover_bytes)\r\n 295: \r\n 296:     with pytest.raises(InvariantViolation) as caught:\r\n 297:         recovery.restore_database(backup, backup_digest, blocked)\r\n 298:     assert str(caught.value).startswith(\"ERR_RESTORE_TARGET_EXISTS\")\r\n 299:     assert blocked.is_dir(), \"the refused resume replaced the interrupted target\"\r\n 300:     assert leftover.read_bytes() == leftover_bytes, (\r\n 301:         \"the refused resume wrote into the interrupted target\"\r\n 302:     )\r\n 303: \r\n 304:     shutil.rmtree(blocked)\r\n 305:     resumed_dir_target = recovery.restore_database(backup, backup_digest, blocked)\r\n 306:     assert resumed_dir_target[\"status\"] == \"RESTORE_READBACK_PASS\"\r\n 307:     assert blocked.is_file()\r\n 308:     assert blocked.read_bytes() == backup_bytes, (\r\n 309:         \"the resumed restore is not byte-identical to the backup\"\r\n 310:     )\r\n 311:     assert _sha256(blocked) == backup_digest\r\n 312:     assert _rows(blocked) == PAYLOADS\r\n 313: \r\n 314:     # Resuming did not consume or rewrite the backup either.\r\n 315:     assert _sha256(backup) == backup_digest\r\n 316:     assert backup.read_bytes() == backup_bytes\r\n 317: \r\n 318: \r\n 319: @pytest.mark.xfail(\r\n 320:     strict=True,\r\n 321:     reason=\"R15_1#1418 operation_id / readback reconciliation absent\",\r\n 322: )\r\n 323: def test_resume_requires_operation_identity(tmp_path):\r\n 324:     \"\"\"A resume has to be addressed by the ``operation_id`` of the attempt it continues.\r\n 325: \r\n 326:     Citation: KB ``SBL_HGK_FABRIC_PIPD_LS_PKG_R15_1...#1418`` -- an effect that timed out or lost\r\n 327:     its transport must not be blindly re-sent; ``operation_id`` is the unique idempotency key and a\r\n 328:     Native state readback comes first. ``CONFIRMED_EFFECT`` continues with verification only,\r\n 329:     ``CONFIRMED_NO_EFFECT`` re-requests under the owner's policy, and ``UNKNOWN_EFFECT`` /\r\n 330:     ``PARTIAL_EFFECT`` pause the affected operation into reconciliation/compensation.\r\n 331: \r\n 332:     ``hg_kseos.recovery`` currently has none of that: ``restore_database`` takes no ``operation_id``\r\n 333:     and its receipts carry no identity, so a caller cannot say which attempt a resume belongs to,\r\n 334:     cannot read the target back to classify a timed-out restore, and therefore cannot tell a\r\n 335:     confirmed effect from an unknown one. This test states the contract so the gap is a named\r\n 336:     failure rather than an unwritten expectation; it is ``xfail(strict=True)`` because the product\r\n 337:     rule is absent, and it will XPASS -- failing loudly -- as soon as the rule arrives, at which\r\n 338:     point the marker must be removed deliberately.\r\n 339:     \"\"\"\r\n 340:     database = _seeded_database(tmp_path)\r\n 341:     receipt = recovery.backup_database(database, tmp_path / \"backups\")\r\n 342:     assert \"operation_id\" in receipt, (\r\n 343:         \"a checkpoint that can be resumed is not addressed by an operation_id\"\r\n 344:     )\r\n 345:     backup = Path(receipt[\"backup\"])\r\n 346:     target = tmp_path / \"restore\" / \"spine.db\"\r\n 347:     operation_id = \"op-resume-0001\"\r\n 348: \r\n 349:     parameters = inspect.signature(recovery.restore_database).parameters\r\n 350:     assert \"operation_id\" in parameters, (\r\n 351:         \"restore_database cannot be told which operation it resumes, so a timed-out restore can \"\r\n 352:         \"only be classified by guessing; #1418 requires the operation_id and a readback first\"\r\n 353:     )\r\n 354: \r\n 355:     first = recovery.restore_database(\r\n 356:         backup, receipt[\"sha256\"], target, operation_id=operation_id\r\n 357:     )\r\n 358:     assert first.get(\"operation_id\") == operation_id, (\r\n 359:         \"the restore receipt does not record the operation it served\"\r\n 360:     )\r\n 361: \r\n 362:     # A readback under the same operation continues a CONFIRMED_EFFECT; it must not become a\r\n 363:     # second application of it.\r\n 364:     readback = recovery.restore_database(\r\n 365:         backup, receipt[\"sha256\"], target, operation_id=operation_id\r\n 366:     )\r\n 367:     assert readback[\"status\"] == \"RESTORE_READBACK_PASS\"\r\n 368:     assert readback.get(\"operation_id\") == operation_id\r\n 369:     assert readback[\"sha256\"] == receipt[\"sha256\"]\r\n 370:     assert _sha256(target) == receipt[\"sha256\"]\r\n 371:     assert _rows(target) == PAYLOADS\r\n 372: \r\n 373:     # A different operation against a target another operation already owns is UNKNOWN/PARTIAL\r\n 374:     # reconciliation territory, not a silent overwrite.\r\n 375:     with pytest.raises(InvariantViolation) as caught:\r\n 376:         recovery.restore_database(\r\n 377:             backup, receipt[\"sha256\"], target, operation_id=\"op-resume-0002\"\r\n 378:         )\r\n 379:     assert str(caught.value).startswith(\r\n 380:         (\"ERR_RESUME_OPERATION_MISMATCH\", \"ERR_RESTORE_TARGET_EXISTS\")\r\n 381:     )\r\n 382:     assert _rows(target) == PAYLOADS\r\n--- C:/Projects/Agent_Workspace/HG-KSEOS/tests/test_c15_evolution_holdout.py\r\n   1: \"\"\"C15: the holdout is not the tuning input, and a failed signal cannot promote.\r\n   2: \r\n   3: What ``hg_kseos.evolution`` actually guarantees, read from the module before this file was written:\r\n   4: \r\n   5: * No holdout set lives on the controller. ``GovernedEvolutionController.__init__(spine,\r\n   6:   event_log=None, recurrence_threshold=2)`` keeps the spine, the log and the recurrence threshold\r\n   7:   and nothing else -- there is no ``holdout`` attribute and no holdout constructor argument. The\r\n   8:   brief's first branch assumed such an attribute exists; it does not, so this file takes the\r\n   9:   documented second branch and asserts what the shipped code really offers:\r\n  10: \r\n  11:   - ``qualify()`` requires ``holdout_pass`` as a keyword-only argument with **no default**, so the\r\n  12:     promotion path cannot run without an explicit holdout verdict. Omitting it raises ``TypeError``\r\n  13:     from the signature instead of silently reading as a pass.\r\n  14:   - the evaluation plan seeded by ``create_candidate()`` carries ``holdout`` as its own channel\r\n  15:     (``{\"tests\": [], \"holdout\": [], \"negative\": [], \"security\": []}``), while the tuning input\r\n  16:     handed to the same call lands in a different column, ``baseline_metrics_json``.\r\n  17:     That channel is seeded EMPTY, and an empty set intersects nothing, so a disjointness claim read\r\n  18:     off the shipped seeding alone is vacuous. DOC-13#727 requires the holdout to be frozen before\r\n  19:     candidate generation with checker-held truth, and requires a leak to surface as\r\n  20:     ``HOLDOUT_CONTAMINATED``. This test therefore freezes a NON-EMPTY holdout first, refuses to make\r\n  21:     any disjointness claim until ``len(holdout) > 0`` is asserted, and then probes contamination\r\n  22:     detection. The probe is expected to fail on the shipped module, which exposes no holdout and no\r\n  23:     contamination check at all; the test is marked ``xfail(strict=True)`` so that gap stays visible\r\n  24:     instead of being papered over by a vacuously true assertion.\r\n  25:   - ``qualify(..., tests_pass=True, holdout_pass=False)`` still rejects, so the holdout verdict is a\r\n  26:     real veto over a candidate that already passed the tuning-facing tests.\r\n  27: \r\n  28:   No holdout API is invented here: every assertion above is read off the shipped signature, schema\r\n  29:   and behaviour. The frozen holdout below is written to the persisted plan column as checker-held\r\n  30:   truth because the controller offers no freeze entry point; that absence is itself part of the\r\n  31:   finding, not a licence to assert against an empty set.\r\n  32: \r\n  33: * The module has no signal-to-promotion entry point. ``promotion_gate()`` keys on a\r\n  34:   ``candidate_id``, and the only signal intake is ``capture_signal()``. A receipt that serves an\r\n  19: arm absent from the pool but is dated at or after the pool revision is still a\r\n  20: VIOLATION -- the rebind only excuses turns taken before it.\r\n  21: \r\n  22: Time comes from the receipt's own `generated_utc` (falling back to the receipt file\r\n  23: mtime) and from the pool file mtime. `classify_arm()` is the pure predicate that\r\n  24: makes this decision, so it is unit-testable with injected values.\r\n  25: \r\n  26: Claim ceiling: the rule compares an arm identity and two timestamps; it does not\r\n  27: prove the served arm appeared in an earlier revision of the pool file. The earlier version of this test deep-copied the\r\n378:     projection before mutating it, which meant an implementation that returned aliases into its own\r\n379:     registry still passed -- the copy absorbed the mutation. That was not a test of the\r\n380:     defensive-copy boundary at all.\r\n381: \r\n382:     FINDING (recorded here rather than reverted, per the repair brief): ``_compact_route`` deep-copies\r\n383:     ``arms`` but builds ``fallbacks`` with ``list(route.get(\"fallbacks\", []))``. A list copy is\r\n384:     shallow, so every projected route's fallback entries ARE the registry's own dict objects. Editing\r\n385:     one fallback dict through a projection therefore rewrites the in-memory registry, and every later\r\n386:     ``project_policy`` call republishes the attacker's fallback. ``arms`` are safe; ``fallbacks`` are\r\n387:     not. The return value is the manifest plus a ``manifest`` path key.\r\n10: * ``restore_database(backup, expected_sha256, restore_target)`` is the resume surface, and it is a\r\n11:   *gate* rather than an overwriting writer. It refuses a digest mismatch with\r\n12:   ``ERR_BACKUP_DIGEST_MISMATCH`` before it touches the filesystem, and it refuses an existing target\r\n13:   with ``ERR_RESTORE_TARGET_EXISTS``. That second refusal is how \"a resume must not repeat a world\r\n14:   effect\" is enforced here: replaying a verified restore does not rewrite, merge into, or accumulate\r\n15:   onto the target -- it is rejected, and the target's bytes stay byte-identical.\r\n16:   The replay test therefore asserts the *refusal plus byte equality* guarantee that the code\r\n17:   documents, not an in-place idempotent rewrite that ``restore_database`` does not implement. If the\r\n18:   implementation ever learns to re-restore in place, that test fails loudly and the guarantee has to\r\n19:   be restated deliberately instead of silently drifting.\r\n20: * A successful restore returns ``RESTORE_READBACK_PASS`` with the sha256 it measured *after* the\r\n21:   copy, and a copy that fails ``PRAGMA integrity_check`` is unlinked before\r\n22:   ``ERR_RESTORE_INTEGRITY`` is raised -- so a failed restore cannot leave a half-written target\r\n23:   standing in for a committed checkpoint.\r\n24: * An interrupted restore is modelled the way a killed copy really leaves the world: the target path\r\n25:   is *occupied* by what the dead attempt produced -- a truncated file, or a directory wearing the\r\n26:   target's name -- and the module reports it as ``ERR_RESTORE_TARGET_EXISTS`` instead of writing\r\n27:   through it. That is a different situation from an absent target, and the resume test pins it.\r\n28: * The module has no ``operation_id``, no readback after a timeout, and no UNKNOWN/PARTIAL\r\n29:   reconciliation (KB ``SBL_HGK_FABRIC_PIPD_LS_PKG_R15_1...#1418``).\r\n30:   ``test_resume_requires_operation_identity`` states that contract and is marked\r\n31:   ``xfail(strict=True)``: it fails today, so the gap stays visible, and it turns XPASS -- and\r\n32:   therefore a failing build -- the moment ``recovery`` grows the identity, so the rule cannot be\r\n33:   quietly satisfied by a weaker reading later.\r\n34: \r\n35: Each test builds its own SQLite database under pytest's ``tmp_path``; there is no network, no repo\r\n36: fixture, and no write outside the pytest temp \"\"\"A resume whose expected digest disagrees with the backup is refused and writes nothing.\r\n180: \r\n181:     Two cases are pinned, because \"untouched\" means different things for each: a test_resume_after_interrupted_restore_reproduces_the_backup_exactly(tmp_path):\r\n219:     \"\"\"After an interrupted restore, resuming from the intact backup reproduces its bytes exactly.\r\n220: \r\n221:     The interruption is not \"the target never existed\". A copy that dies part-way leaves the target\r\n222:     path *occupied* by its own debris, and this test produces both shapes of that debris: a file\r\n223:     holding a strict prefix of the backup (a killed ``copy2``), and a directory sitting on the\r\n224:     target name with a partial payload inside (a crashed extraction). In each case the module\r\n225:     reports the occupied destination -- ``ERR_RESTORE_TARGET_EXISTS`` -- and leaves the debris\r\n226:     byte-identical, so the resume cannot merge into it, append to it, or silently treat a\r\n227:     half-written file as a checkpoint. Once the failed attempt's debris is cleared, the resume has\r\n228:     to reproduce the backup: the load-bearing assertions are byte equality with the backup file,\r\n229:     its digest, ``PRAGMA integrity_check``, and each row present exactly once -- not any message.\r\n230:     \"\"\"\r\n231:     database = _seeded_database(tmp_path)\r\n232:     receipt = recovery.backup_database(database, tmp_path / \"backups\")\r\n233:     backup = Path(receipt[\"backup\"])\r\n234:     backup_digest = _sha256(backup)\r\n235:     backup_bytes = backup.read_bytes()\r\n236:     assert backup_digest == receipt[\"sha256\"]\r\n237: \r\n238:     # scope/risk/budget/expiry => HUMAN_DECISION with DecisionPack, zero effect\r\n1409: IF permitted READ_ONLY_RESEARCH => can retry bounded query/evaluation, not native effect\r\n1410: IF REPAIR_SCOPED => issue HGK Native WorkOrder request; only Native APL + live permit may admit\r\n1411: ELSE STOP_AFFECTED_TASK, preserve other independent Runnable cards\r\n1412: ```\r\n1413: \r\n1414: FAR 的自動採納不是「LLM 說 PASS 就批准」；每項推薦帶 `recommendation_id, finding_id, policy_digest, affected_scope, requested_class, expected_evidence, first_fail, owner, decision, record_at`。任何 `UNKNOWN/STALE` 證據、成本上限已滿、許可剛遭撤回、跨分支 SHA、模型自稱可寫，都必 `HOLD/STOP`。已合法且仍在 Standby Grant 內的 ordinary repair 可以不逐件詢問，但仍每次受 HGK Native 合法簽發的 WO/Permit 控制。\r\n1415: \r\n1416: ### 12.4 Effect 僅能 at-most-once；與 research/decision retry 完全不同\r\n1417: \r\n1418: 研究與反覆推理可在 bounded retries 下生成新的**提案**；effect 若 timeout/transport error，不得盲目重送寫入：`operation_id` 做唯一 idempotency key，必先 Native state readback。`CONFIRMED_EFFECT` → 只續後驗證；`CONFIRMED_NO_EFFECT` → 依 Native Owner 有效策略重新請求，不自動複用過期 token；`UNKNOWN_EFFECT/PARTIAL_EFFECT` → 暫停受影 operation、隔離工作樹、進入 reconciliation/compensation；`STALE_PERMIT` → DENY。PIPD ECP reference 合約 `retry max1, backoff none, at-most-once` 不可被 FAR 的研究重試迴圈覆蓋。\r\n1419: \r\n1420: ### 12.5 Hermes 真 Kanban/Swarm + durable checkpoints / workload lease renewal\r\n1421: \r\n1422: ```yaml\r\n1423: kind:
